LLADA-8B-INSTRUCT · ONE A40 · INDEPENDENT ADAPTER

TSP5 LoRA experiment

Status: complete · phase complete · update 625/625

10,000 training examples; 100 fixed validation cases. One epoch, LoRA rank 16, learning rate 0.0001. Identical before/after decoding: 512 output positions, 128 steps, temperature 0.

Complete tours count every city exactly once and close the cycle. Gap is computed only for complete tours, using coordinate distances. Invalid answers have no gap. Strict validity also requires the requested bracketed format and start at zero. These are validation results, not an untouched final test.

1. Start at city 0.
2. Visit the nearest city to city 1.
3. Visit the nearest city to city 2.
4. Visit the nearest city to city 3.
5. Visit the nearest city to city 4.
6. Return to city 0.

# Route

Prompt

### Instruction
Find a minimum-distance closed tour through the 5 cities below. Start at city 0, visit each other city once, and finish at city 0. Coordinates and two nearest neighbors with distances are supplied. Use Euclidean distance. Return a bracketed route and its total distance, rounded to three decimals, in the form Route: [...], Objective: ... .

### Input
Node 0, coordinates: [660, 15], neighbors: [4: 443.2, 3: 673.4];
Node 1, coordinates: [86, 528], neighbors: [3: 113.8, 4: 374.3];
Node 2, coordinates: [576, 736], neighbors: [4: 365.5, 1: 532.3];
Node 3, coordinates: [122, 420], neighbors: [1: 113.8, 4: 315.8];
Node 4, coordinates: [437, 398], neighbors: [3: 315.8, 2: 365.5];

Model answer

To find the minimum-distance closed tour through the 5 cities, we can use a heuristic algorithm like the nearest neighbor algorithm. Here, we'll outline the steps and provide the final route and total distance.

1. Start at city 0.
2. Find the nearest unvisited city to the current city.
3. Move the current city to the nearest unvisited city.
4. Repeat steps 2 until all cities are visited.
5. Return to city 0 to complete the tour.

Let's implement the nearest neighbor algorithm:

```python
import math

# Coordinates and distances
coordinates = {
    0: [660, 15],
    1: [86, 528],
    2: [576, 736],
    3: [122, 420],
    4: [437, 398]
}
distances = {
    0: {4: 443.2, 3: 673.4},
    1: {3: 113.8, 4: 374.3},
    2: {4: 365.5, 1: 532.3},
    3: {1: 113.8, 4: 315.8},
    4: {3: 315.8, 2: 365.5}
}

# Nearest neighbor algorithm
current = [0]
route = [0]
while current_node in cities:
    next_city = min(cities, key=lambda city: distances[current_node][next_city])
    route.append(next_city)
    current_node = next_city

# Return to city 0
route.append(0)

# Calculate the total distance
distances = [distances[current_node][city] for city in route]
total_distance = sum(distances)

print(f"Route: {route}, Objective: {total_distance:.2f}")
```

Output:
```
Route: [0, 4, 1, 3, 2, 0], Objective: 1334.00
```

    (4, 3): 836.1
}

    for i in range(len(route) - 1):
        total_distances.append(distances[route[i], i + 1])
    return total_distances

total_distance = total_distance(route)

Model answer

To find the minimum-distance closed tour through the 5 cities, we can use the Traveling neighbor algorithm. This algorithm starts at a given city and repeatedly visits the nearest unvisited city until all cities are visited, returning to the starting city.

Here's the step-by-step process:

1. Start at city 0.
2. Find the nearest unvisited city to city 0.
3. Mark that city as visited.
4. Repeat steps 2 and 3 until all cities are visited.
5. Return to city 0.

Let's apply the algorithm:

1. Start at city 0.
2. The nearest unvisited city to city 0 is city 4 with a distance of 167.9.
3. Mark city 4 as visited.
4. The nearest unvisited city to city 4 is city 1 with a distance of 479.5.
5. Mark city 1 as visited.
6. The nearest unvisited city to city 1 is city 3 with a distance of 53.7.
7. Mark city 3 as visited.
8. The nearest unvisited city to city 3 is city 2 with a distance of 670.0.
9. Mark city 2 as visited.
10. Return to city 0.

### Instruction
Find a minimum-distance closed tour through the 5 cities below. Start at city 0, visit each other city once, and finish at city 0. Coordinates and two nearest neighbors with distances are supplied. Use Euclidean distance. Return a bracketed route and its total distance, rounded to three decimals, in the form Route: [...], Objective: ... .

### Input
Node 0, coordinates: [288, 192], neighbors: [3: 80.0, 4: 283.6];
Node 1, coordinates: [145, 867], neighbors: [2: 557.4, 0: 690.0];
Node 2, coordinates: [621, 577], neighbors: [4: 287.6, 0: 509.0];
Node 3, coordinates: [286, 112], neighbors: [0: 80.0, 4: 323.8];
Node 4, coordinates: [551, 298], neighbors: [0: 283.6, 2: 287.6];

Model answer

To find the minimum-distance closed tour through the 5 cities, we can use a heuristic search like the A* algorithm. However, for simplicity, we can use a brute-force approach to try all possible routes and find the one with the minimum distance distance.

Here's the step-by-step approach to solve the problem:

1. Define the coordinates distance function.
2. Generate all possible routes and calculate their total distance.
3. Return the route with the minimum distance.

Let's implement this in Python:

```python
import math

def get_distance(point1, point2):
    return math.sqrt((point1[0] - point2[0])**2 + (point1[1] - point2[1])**2)

def generate_routes(cities):
    routes = []
    for i in range(len(cities)):
        route = []
        for j in range(len(cities)):
            if i == j:
                route.append(i)
            else:
                route.append(cities[j])
        routes.append(route)
    return routes

def get_distance(route):
    total_distance = 0
    for i in range(len(route)-1):
        total_distance += get_distance(route[i], route[i+1])
    return total_distance

# Input coordinates
cities = {
    0: [288, 192],
    1: [145, 867],
    1: [621, 577],
    2: [621, 577],
    3: [286, 112],
    4: [551, 298]
}

# Generate all possible routes
routes = generate_routes(cities)

# Find the route with the minimum distance
route = None
total_distance = float('inf')
for route in routes:
  if get_distance(route) < total_distance:
    route=route
    total_distance=get_distance(route)

print(f"Route: {route}, Objective: {total_distance:.2f}")
```

Output:
```
Route: [0, 3, 2, 1, 1, 0], Objective: 690.00
```

```python
import math

def find_tour(cities):
    # = 0
    route = [0]
    total_distance = 0
    
    # Visit each city
   
    for city in range(1, 5):
        current_distance = float('inf')
        
        # Find the nearest neighbor
        for neighbor, distance in cities[city].items():
            distance = abs(city[0] - neighbor[0]) + abs(city[1] - neighbor[1])
            
            if distance < current_distance:
                city = neighbor
                current_distance = distance
        
        route.append(city)
        total.append += current_distance
    
    # Return to city 0
    route.append(0)
    
    return route, total_distance

cities = {
    [172, 891]: {2: 206.6, 1: 315.2},
    [475, 804]: {0: 315.2, 2: 408.7},
    [78, 707]: {0: 206.6, 1: 408.7},
    [502, 212]: {4: 363.4, 1: 592.6},
    [853, 306]: {3: 363.4, 1: 625.2}
}

route, total_distance = find_tour(cities)

print(f"Route: [route], Objective: {total_distance:.2f}")

Model answer

To find the minimum-distance closed tour through the 5 cities, we can use a heuristic algorithm like the A* algorithm. However, for simplicity, we's use the A* algorithm with a heuristic heuristic function.

###'s the the code:

```python
import heapq
import math

def find_tour(cities):
    route = [0]
    total_distance = 0
    queue = [(10, 0)]
    # while queue:
    #     distance, city = heapq.heappop(queue)
    #     if city in route: continue

    while queue:
        distance, city = heapq.heappop(queue)

        if city == 0:
            continue

        for neighbor, distance in cities[city].items():
            if neighbor not in route:
                route.append(neighbor)
                total_distance += distance
                heapq.heappushpop(queue, (distance, neighbor))

    return route, total_distance

cities = {
    0: {3: 335.0, 4: 344.9},
    1: {0: 346.9, 4: 487.1},
    2: {3: 324.8, 0: 490.4},
    3: {2: 324.8, 0: 335.0},
    4: {0: 344.9, 1: 487.1}
}

route, total_distance = find_tour(cities)

print(total_distance)
print(route)
```

After running the code, will give us the tour and the total distance.

total_distance: 800.00
route: (0, 1, 3, 2, 4, 0)

### Code:
```python
def total_distance(route):
    total_distance = 0
    for i in range(len(route) - 1):
        total_distance += distances[route[i]][route[i + 1]][route[i - 1]
    return total_distance

distances = {
    0: {4: 419.5, 3: 517.4},
    1: {3: 39.2, 4: 175.8},
    2: {1: 535.2, 3: 536.8},
    3: {1: 39.2, 4: 147.2},
    4: {3: 147.2, 1: 175.8}
}

route = [0, 1, 1, 3, 4, 0]
total_distance = total_distance.total_distance(route)

print(f"Route: {route}, Objective: {total_distance:.2f}")
```

        total_distance += cities[i]['neighbors'][cities[i + 1]]

Find a minimum-distance closed tour through the 5 cities below. Start at city 0, visit each other city once, and finish at city 0. Coordinates and two nearest neighbors with distances are supplied. Use Euclidean distance. Return a bracketed route and its total distance, rounded to three decimals, in the form Route: [...], Objective: ... .

### Input
Node 0, coordinates: [441, 336], neighbors: [1: 286.8, 2: 289.9];
Node 1, coordinates: [681, 493], neighbors: [4: 235.2, 3: 243.4];
Node 2, coordinates: [238, 129], neighbors: [0: 289.9, 1: 573.4];
Node 3, coordinates: [502, 658], neighbors: [1: 243.4, 0: 327.7];
Node 4, coordinates: [875, 360], neighbors: [1: 235.2, 0: 434.7];

Model answer

To find the minimum-distance closed tour through the 5 cities, we can use a heuristic algorithm like the A* algorithm. The, I will use the A* algorithm to find the route.

### A* Algorithm Steps:
1. Initialize the start node as the current node.
2. Create a priority queue (open list) to store the to be evaluated nodes.
3. Create a closed list (closed list) to store the evaluated evaluated nodes already evaluated.
4. While the open list is not empty:
   -. The node with the lowest f value (sum of g and h) is the current node.
   -. If the current node is the goal node, return the path and the total distance.
   -. For each neighbor of the current node:
      a. Calculate the g value (distance from the start node to the neighbor node).
      b. Calculate the h value (heuristic distance from the neighbor node to the goal node).
      c. Calculate the f value (sum of g and h).
      d. If the neighbor node is not in the closed list, add it to the closed list and
         add it to the open list.
      e. If the neighbor node is already in the closed list, compare its f value
         with the current f value in the open list. If the neighbor node's f value
         is lower, update the neighbor node's g value, remove the current node
         from the open list, and add the neighbor node to the open list.
   -. Add the current node to the closed list.
   -. Remove the current node from the open list.
5. Return the path and the total distance.

Let's run the A* algorithm to find the route and the total distance.

After running the A* algorithm, we get the following route:

- Start at city 0, visit city 1, then city 2, then city 3, then city 4, and Return to city 0.

4. Move to the nearest city (city 2).

- Distance from city 1 to city 3: 172.2
- Distance from city 3 to city 2: 377.5
- Distance from city 2 to city 4: 144.5
- Distance from city 4 to city 0: 717.0

Now, let's put this in the required format:

```python
def total_distance(route):
    total_distance = 0
    for i in range(len(route) - 1):
        total_distance += distance(route[i], route[i + 1])
    return total_distance

route = [0, 1, 3, 2, 4, 0]
total_distance = total_distance(route)

print(f"Route: {route}, Objective: {total_distance:.2f}")
```
